  Exemplars: Anne Lamott's Traveling Mercies; Mary Karr's Lit; Caroline Knapp's Drinking: A Love Story; Augusten Burroughs's Dry; Twelve-step recovery memoirs.

GENRE 545: Food Memoir
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Food memory or meal
    2. Family and cultural context
    3. Cooking process or eating ritual
    4. Personal life events tied to food
    5. Climactic meal or recipe
    6. Recipe shared as gift
  Description: Memoir whose primary frame is food — meals, cooking, family recipes, restaurant work. Food as structural through-line.
  Exemplars: Ruth Reichl's memoirs; M.F.K. Fisher; Anthony Bourdain's Kitchen Confidential; Madhur Jaffrey; Heat (Buford).

GENRE 546: Public Intellectual Essay
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Topic of public significance introduced
    2. Author's authority/perspective established
    3. Detailed argument with examples
    4. Engagement with counterpositions
    5. Climactic claim
    6. Implications for public life
  Description: Long-form essay by a public intellectual — combining argument, scholarship, and broad cultural-political address. Expert-voice authority and ambition for general audience.
  Exemplars: Hannah Arendt's essays; James Baldwin's longer essays; Susan Sontag; Edward Said; Cornel West; Ta-Nehisi Coates.

GENRE 547: Polemic
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Position stated forcefully
    2. Antagonist position named
    3. Sustained refutation
    4. Positive case advanced with intensity
    5. Climactic confrontation
    6. Call to action or commitment
  Description: Strong, often combative argumentative essay or book. Passionate, confrontational rhetorical register and explicit advocacy.
  Exemplars: Christopher Hitchens's polemics; Tom Paine's Common Sense; Frederick Douglass; bell hooks's polemic essays; Andrea Dworkin.

GENRE 548: Diary / Journal (literary)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Date and entry
    2. Day's events or thoughts
    3. Voice consistent across entries
    4. Patterns emerge over time
    5. Major life events recorded as they happen
    6. Reader watches life unfold without retrospect
  Description: Day-by-day or entry-by-entry first-person record kept across time, sometimes published. Real-time, accumulative structure rather than retrospective shaping.
  Exemplars: Samuel Pepys's Diary; Anne Frank's Diary; Susan Sontag's journals; Virginia Woolf's diaries; Joan Didion's notebooks.

GENRE 549: Letter (literary, public)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Salutation to specific addressee
    2. Direct, intimate register
    3. Specific subject taken up
    4. Personal disclosure as part of argument
    5. Climactic moment of address
    6. Closing
  Description: Open or published letter as literary form — addressed to a specific recipient but designed for public reading. Rhetorical-public ambition.
  Exemplars: Martin Luther King Jr.'s "Letter from a Birmingham Jail"; James Baldwin's "Letter to My Nephew"; Ta-Nehisi Coates's Between the World and Me.

GENRE 550: Annotated Edition / Marginalia
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Primary text presented
    2. Annotations begin to comment
    3. Annotations build their own argument
    4. Tension between text and annotation
    5. Annotation's voice climaxes
    6. Reader synthesizes text and commentary
  Description: Critical apparatus — extensive footnotes or commentary — that creates a parallel narrative alongside a primary text. Dialogue-with-text register.
  Exemplars: Vladimir Nabokov's Pale Fire (fictionalized variant); Pope's The Dunciad Variorum; Talmudic commentary; David Foster Wallace's annotated essays.

---

# ADDITIONAL JOURNALISM (deep)

GENRE 551: Wire Report
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Lead with most critical fact
    2. Source or location
    3. Brief context
    4. Closing factual detail
  Description: Brief, fact-focused dispatch from a wire service designed for syndication — fastest hard news. Extreme brevity and absolute factual register.
  Exemplars: AP and Reuters wire service reports; AFP dispatches; Bloomberg flash reports.

GENRE 552: Beat Reporting
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Topic or institution covered
    2. Sources cultivated over time
    3. Specific story emerges from beat-knowledge
    4. Insider context provided
    5. Story advances ongoing narrative
    6. Closing places story in beat-context
  Description: Sustained coverage of a specific topic-area (politics, courts, education) by a single reporter who develops sources and expertise. Topic-depth.
  Exemplars: Beat reporters at major newspapers; Politico's beats; specific political reporters (Maggie Haberman).

GENRE 553: Enterprise Journalism
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Reporter notices anomaly or pattern
    2. Initial reporting confirms there's a story
    3. Sustained investigation
    4. Sources and documents accumulated
    5. Pattern revealed
    6. Story published with original framing
  Description: Original, self-initiated journalism that goes beyond breaking-news coverage — typically discovering or framing a story not on the daily agenda. Initiative-driven.
  Exemplars: ProPublica investigations; NYT enterprise pieces; investigative team work at major outlets.

GENRE 554: Solutions Journalism
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Problem named with specificity
    2. Solution or intervention introduced
    3. How it works in practice
    4. Evidence of effectiveness
    5. Limitations and unanswered questions
    6. Implications and replicability
  Description: Reporting that focuses on responses to social problems — what's working, what's being tried, with rigorous examination. Investigative rigor and attention to evidence.
  Exemplars: Solutions Journalism Network archive; Christian Science Monitor's solution-oriented reporting; YES! Magazine; NYT "Fixes" column.

GENRE 555: Hyperlocal Journalism
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Local situation framed
    2. Neighborhood-specific context
    3. Local people named and quoted
    4. Specific local stakes
    5. Resolution at hyperlocal scale
    6. Implications for the community
  Description: Reporting at the level of a single neighborhood, community, or small town — covering local politics, schools, businesses, characters that wouldn't make regional or national news.
  Exemplars: Patch.com (early years); West Seattle Blog; Curbed; local weekly papers generally.

GENRE 556: Data Journalism
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Dataset or data question introduced
    2. Methodology explained
    3. Findings presented (often with visualization)
    4. Pattern revealed
    5. Implications drawn
    6. Story humanized through specific cases
  Description: Reporting in which data analysis IS the journalism — patterns in datasets reveal stories that would not be apparent through interview-based reporting.
  Exemplars: Nate Silver's election work; NYT's "The Upshot"; FiveThirtyEight; ProPublica's data investigations.

GENRE 557: Constructive Journalism
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Story tied to civic significance
    2. Multiple perspectives included
    3. Context for understanding
    4. Pathways for engagement or action
    5. Resources or next steps
    6. Audience addressed as participants
  Description: Reporting that explicitly aims at productive outcomes — engaged democracy, problem-solving, audience empowerment — rather than just informing or critiquing.
  Exemplars: Solutions Journalism Network; YES! Magazine; some BBC and NPR civic series.

GENRE 558: Slow Journalism / Long-Read
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Patient observation begins
    2. Sources cultivated over time
    3. Layers of complexity emerge
    4. Connections recognized that wouldn't appear in fast coverage
    5. Climactic insight
    6. Long, careful publication
  Description: Reporting that takes weeks or months and publishes as long-form analysis or narrative. Temporal investment.
  Exemplars: Delayed Gratification magazine; The Atlantic's long features; New Yorker reportage; Granta.

GENRE 559: Magazine Feature
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Vivid lead scene or anecdote
    2. Nut graf signaling significance
    3. Body building character and context
    4. Multiple scenes
    5. Climactic moment or revelation
    6. Closing kicker
  Description: 3,000-10,000 word feature article — bridges journalism and literary nonfiction. Mid-length and magazine-rhythm pacing.
  Exemplars: New Yorker features; Atlantic features; Vanity Fair; GQ features; Tom Wolfe's New Journalism.

GENRE 560: Newsletter (modern)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Subject line or open hook
    2. Brief intro/orientation
    3. Main content (analysis, links, story)
    4. Author's distinctive voice throughout
    5. Closing reflection or sign-off
    6. Direct reader address
  Description: Email-distributed personal or specialized publication — combining curation, analysis, and reporting in distinctive single-author voice. Direct-to-reader, voice-driven model.
  Exemplars: Substack newsletters generally; Stratechery (Ben Thompson); Letters from an American (Heather Cox Richardson); Slow Boring (Yglesias).

GENRE 561: Press Release Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Headline with hook
    2. Lead with key fact
    3. Quote from executive
    4. Background and context
    5. Additional quotes
    6. Boilerplate company description
  Description: Institutional announcement structured as if it were a news story — designed to be repurposed by journalists. Self-promotional but news-format register.
  Exemplars: Apple press releases; PR Newswire distributions; standard corporate-communications announcements.

GENRE 562: Photo Essay
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Opening image establishes subject
    2. Successive images build narrative
    3. Telling details revealed visually
    4. Climactic image
    5. Resolution or close image
    6. Captions support without dominating
  Description: Story told primarily through photographs with minimal accompanying text. Images get structural primacy.
  Exemplars: W. Eugene Smith's photo essays in Life; Sebastião Salgado's projects; National Geographic photo essays; Robert Frank's The Americans.

GENRE 563: Long-Form Podcast Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Episode 1 introduces subject and stakes
    2. Each episode advances investigation/story
    3. Recurring narrator voice and structure
    4. Episode cliffhangers
    5. Climactic episode delivers payoff
    6. Closing episode reflects on the journey
  Description: Multi-episode podcast told as a single sustained narrative — investigative, biographical, or thematic. Serial-narrative structure.
  Exemplars: Serial; S-Town; The Daily; This American Life's longer projects; Slow Burn.

GENRE 564: News Conference / Press Conference
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Opening statement read
    2. Specific announcement or position
    3. Q&A begins
    4. Pointed questions, evasions or admissions
    5. Climactic exchange
    6. Closing
  Description: Formal address by official to assembled journalists, followed by Q&A. Institutional ritual and adversarial dynamic.
  Exemplars: White House press briefings; corporate press conferences; sports press conferences after major games.

GENRE 565: Sports Recap (game story)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Lead with key result and stakes
    2. Score and game-deciding play
    3. Quotes from key participants
    4. Statistical context
    5. Implications for season or career
    6. Closing scene or quote
  Description: Day-after report of a sporting event — score, key plays, quotes, implications. Specific game-event focus.
  Exemplars: Wire service game stories; ESPN game recaps; daily-newspaper sports sections.

---

# ADDITIONAL BUSINESS / STRATEGY (deep)

GENRE 566: Business Memo
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. To/From/Date/Subject header
    2. Purpose stated in first line
    3. Background or context
    4. Specific information or analysis
    5. Recommendation or call to action
    6. Closing or attachments listed
  Description: Internal corporate communication to colleagues — concise, action-oriented, often template-formatted. Institutional brevity and operational register.
  Exemplars: Standard business memos; Amazon's six-page narrative memos (Bezos); McKinsey internal memos.

GENRE 567: Cold Email Pitch
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Subject line that earns the open
    2. Personalized opener
    3. Brief introduction
    4. Specific value proposition
    5. Clear ask
    6. Easy yes or specific next step
  Description: Outreach email to a stranger — sales, partnership, fundraising — designed to be opened, read, and answered. Extreme brevity and conversion-focus.
  Exemplars: Standard sales-development cold emails; founder-to-investor outreach; modern cold-outreach templates.

GENRE 568: Investor Update
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Headline metrics
    2. Wins of the period
    3. Challenges/lessons
    4. Key initiatives next period
    5. Specific asks (intros, advice)
    6. Closing thanks
  Description: Monthly or quarterly email/letter from founder to investors — metrics, progress, challenges, asks. Accountability-with-momentum register.
  Exemplars: Standard YC and Sequoia-style investor updates; Andrew Chen's writings; modern startup founder convention.

GENRE 569: Board Deck
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Agenda
    2. Performance against plan
    3. Strategic context
    4. Specific decisions sought
    5. Risk and forward outlook
    6. Q&A and decisions logged
  Description: Presentation prepared for a corporate board meeting — quarterly performance, strategic direction, key decisions sought. Formal-governance audience.
  Exemplars: Standard SaaS board decks; PE board materials; standard corporate-governance practice.

GENRE 570: One-Pager / Executive Brief
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Title and TL;DR
    2. Background (one paragraph)
    3. Options (bulleted)
    4. Recommendation
    5. Risk/considerations
    6. Decision required
  Description: Single-page summary of a complex situation or proposal designed for executive consumption — ruthless distillation. Severe brevity and decision-orientation.
  Exemplars: Pentagon brief format; consulting one-pagers; modern enterprise decision documents.

GENRE 571: Business Plan
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Executive summary
    2. Market analysis
    3. Product/service description
    4. Marketing and sales plan
    5. Operations plan
    6. Financial projections and ask
  Description: Comprehensive document outlining a business — market, product, strategy, financials. Formal-comprehensive structure.
  Exemplars: SBA business plan templates; traditional MBA-school format; banking-loan business plans.

GENRE 572: Lean Canvas / Business Model Canvas
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Customer segments
    2. Value proposition
    3. Channels
    4. Customer relationships
    5. Revenue streams
    6. Key resources/activities/partners
    7. Cost structure
  Description: Compressed alternative to traditional business plan — single-page visual format covering customer segments, value proposition, revenue, costs, etc. Template-driven brevity.
  Exemplars: Alex Osterwalder's Business Model Canvas; Ash Maurya's Lean Canvas; Steve Blank's Customer Development.

GENRE 573: Marketing Plan
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Market situation
    2. Target audience and personas
    3. Positioning and messaging
    4. Channels and tactics
    5. Calendar and budget
    6. KPIs and measurement
  Description: Strategic document outlining how a product will reach its market — positioning, channels, campaigns, budget. Marketing-specific focus.
  Exemplars: Standard CMO documents; agency-prepared marketing plans; brand quarterly plans.

GENRE 574: Strategic Plan
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Vision and mission
    2. Strategic context (SWOT, PESTLE)
    3. Multi-year goals
    4. Strategic initiatives
    5. Capabilities required
    6. Measurement and review cadence
  Description: Multi-year direction-setting document for an organization — vision, goals, initiatives, capabilities. Long-horizon, organization-wide scope.
  Exemplars: Corporate 5-year strategic plans; nonprofit strategic plans; government agency strategic plans.

GENRE 575: Vision Document
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Future-state vision
    2. Why this vision matters
    3. What it looks like when achieved
    4. Values that animate it
    5. Path principles (not detailed plan)
    6. Call to alignment
  Description: Aspirational long-term articulation of where an organization wants to be — typically less operational than strategic plan, more inspirational.
  Exemplars: Built to Last (Collins) vision-discussions; founder vision letters; nonprofit vision statements; corporate purpose documents.

GENRE 576: Mission Statement
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Who we serve
    2. What we do for them
    3. Why it matters
  Description: Compressed statement of an organization's purpose — typically one to three sentences. Operational-aspirational hybrid.
  Exemplars: Patagonia's mission; Google's "to organize the world's information"; nonprofit mission statements generally.

GENRE 577: RFP Response (Request for Proposal)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Cover letter and executive summary
    2. Understanding of requirement
    3. Proposed approach
    4. Team and qualifications
    5. Timeline and deliverables
    6. Cost
  Description: Formal proposal responding to a procurement request — typically agency or government. Compliance-driven structure: each requirement must be addressed.
  Exemplars: Federal contract proposals; agency responses to government RFPs; consulting RFP responses.

GENRE 578: Earnings Call Q&A Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Analyst introduces self and firm
    2. Question (often pointed)
    3. Management response
    4. Follow-up if permitted
    5. Next analyst
    6. Closing
  Description: The Q&A portion of an earnings call — analyst questions and management responses. Dynamic adversarial-collegial register.
  Exemplars: Standard earnings call Q&A format; Apple, Tesla earnings calls; sell-side analyst interactions.

GENRE 579: Annual Report (full)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. CEO letter
    2. Strategic overview
    3. Business segment results
    4. Financial statements
    5. Risk factors
    6. Governance and management
  Description: Comprehensive yearly publication summarizing financial performance, narrative, and strategic direction. More comprehensive than shareholder letter.
  Exemplars: Berkshire Hathaway annual reports; modern corporate annual reports; nonprofit annual reports.

GENRE 580: Sustainability / ESG Report
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. CEO/CSR letter
    2. Materiality assessment
    3. Environmental performance
    4. Social impact
    5. Governance practices
    6. Forward commitments and goals
  Description: Corporate report on environmental, social, and governance performance — alongside or separate from financial annual report. Non-financial accountability register.
  Exemplars: Microsoft's sustainability reports; Patagonia's environmental reports; SASB and GRI-aligned reports.

---

# TECH / DIGITAL GENRES

GENRE 581: Bug Report
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Title summarizing the bug
    2. Steps to reproduce
    3. Expected behavior
    4. Actual behavior
    5. Environment details
    6. Severity and assignment
  Description: Documentation of a software defect — what went wrong, how to reproduce, expected vs actual behavior. Specific debugging-utility register.
  Exemplars: GitHub issues; Jira tickets; standard QA bug reports.

GENRE 582: Feature Specification (spec)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Problem and user need
    2. Feature description
    3. User stories or use cases
    4. Detailed requirements
    5. Acceptance criteria
    6. Open questions and dependencies
  Description: Document describing a software feature in detail — for engineers, designers, and stakeholders. Operational, build-this-thing register.
  Exemplars: Standard product spec documents; PRDs (Product Requirements Documents); modern startup spec conventions.

GENRE 583: RFC (Request for Comments)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Problem statement
    2. Proposed solution
    3. Alternatives considered
    4. Risks and tradeoffs
    5. Implementation plan
    6. Open questions for comment
  Description: Engineering proposal document inviting comment from teammates before implementation. Collaborative-review register.
  Exemplars: IETF RFCs (the original); modern engineering RFCs at Google, Stripe, GitLab; Rust RFCs; Python PEPs.

GENRE 584: Technical Postmortem
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Incident summary
    2. Detailed timeline
    3. Root cause
    4. Contributing factors
    5. Action items
    6. Lessons learned
  Description: Engineering analysis of an incident — outage, security breach, data loss — written for organizational learning. Blameless, systems-focused register.
  Exemplars: Google SRE blameless postmortems; AWS post-event summaries; GitLab incident reports; Stripe engineering blog incident write-ups.

GENRE 585: API Documentation
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Authentication overview
    2. Endpoint listing
    3. Per-endpoint: request format
    4. Per-endpoint: response format
    5. Example requests and responses
    6. Error codes
  Description: Reference documentation for software APIs — endpoints, parameters, responses, examples. Strict reference-utility register and example-driven structure.
  Exemplars: Stripe API docs (gold standard); Twilio docs; GitHub REST API docs; OpenAPI specifications.

GENRE 586: Changelog / Release Notes
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Version number and date
    2. New features
    3. Improvements
    4. Bug fixes
    5. Breaking changes
    6. Deprecations and migration notes
  Description: Document recording what changed in a software release — features, fixes, breaking changes. Version-keyed structural register.
  Exemplars: Standard software changelogs; keepachangelog.com convention; npm package release notes; macOS/iOS release notes.

GENRE 587: Engineering Blog Post / Deep Dive
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Hook with concrete problem
    2. System or technology backgrounded
    3. Detailed walk-through
    4. Tradeoffs and decisions
    5. Outcomes and learnings
    6. Future or related work
  Description: Long-form technical blog post explaining how a system, problem, or solution works at depth. Narrative-explanatory register.
  Exemplars: Stripe Engineering blog; Netflix Tech Blog; Cloudflare blog; High Scalability posts; Hacker News-typical deep dives.

GENRE 588: Open Source README
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. What this project is
    2. Why it exists
    3. Installation/quick start
    4. Usage examples
    5. Documentation and links
    6. License and contributing
  Description: Project's primary documentation file — first thing visitors see on a code repository. Onboarding-utility register.
  Exemplars: Standard GitHub README files; popular OSS projects (React, Linux kernel, Rails); make a readme convention.

GENRE 589: Postmortem (product)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Project summary
    2. Goals vs outcomes
    3. What went well
    4. What didn't
    5. Why (root causes)
    6. Action items for next time
  Description: Analysis of a product launch, feature, or campaign after the fact — what worked, what didn't, what to learn. Product-focus rather than technical.
  Exemplars: Standard product retrospectives; agile retrospectives; Fast Company product post-mortems; failure-celebration cultures.

GENRE 590: Internal Engineering Documentation
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. System or process named
    2. Architecture overview
    3. Operations and procedures
    4. Common issues and resolution
    5. Escalation paths
    6. References and history
  Description: Documentation for internal engineering teams — runbooks, architecture docs, system overviews, onboarding guides. Insider audience.
  Exemplars: Internal Confluence pages; Notion engineering wikis; runbooks; on-call documentation.

---

# ADDITIONAL ACADEMIC GENRES (deep)

GENRE 591: Review Article (academic)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Topic and scope
    2. Major theoretical positions
    3. Empirical findings synthesized
    4. Tensions and debates
    5. Open questions
    6. Future directions
  Description: Comprehensive synthesis of research on a topic, distinct from systematic literature review by its narrative scope and authorial perspective.
  Exemplars: Annual Review of Psychology articles; Trends in... journal articles; comprehensive review chapters in handbooks.

GENRE 592: Systematic Review / Meta-Analysis
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Research question and protocol
    2. Search strategy and inclusion criteria
    3. Screening and selection
    4. Data extraction
    5. Quality assessment
    6. Statistical synthesis and conclusions
  Description: Methodologically rigorous review of all existing studies on a question, often combined with statistical meta-analysis. Explicit methodology and reproducibility.
  Exemplars: Cochrane systematic reviews; PRISMA-compliant reviews; meta-analyses in psychology, medicine, education.

GENRE 593: Case Study (academic)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Research question
    2. Case selection rationale
    3. Methodology
    4. Detailed case description
    5. Analytical framework applied
    6. Theoretical contribution
  Description: In-depth examination of a single case, organization, or situation — distinct from teaching case by being scholarly research rather than pedagogy.
  Exemplars: Robert Yin's case study methodology; sociological case studies; policy case studies.

GENRE 594: Theoretical Paper
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Theoretical question
    2. Existing frameworks reviewed
    3. Gaps identified
    4. New theoretical proposition advanced
    5. Implications and predictions
    6. Empirical agenda for testing
  Description: Academic paper advancing a new theoretical framework or refining an existing one — without empirical study. Original theory-building.
  Exemplars: Major theoretical papers in psychology, sociology, economics; foundational papers like Kahneman-Tversky's Prospect Theory.

GENRE 595: Methodology Paper
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Methodological gap or need
    2. Existing methods reviewed
    3. New method proposed
    4. Validation evidence
    5. Application examples
    6. Recommendations for use
  Description: Paper introducing a new research methodology, statistical technique, or instrument. Focuses on the how rather than the substantive findings.
  Exemplars: Papers introducing new statistical techniques; psychometric instrument validation papers; field-research-method papers.

GENRE 596: Replication Study
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Original study summarized
    2. Importance of replicating stated
    3. Method (matching or modifying original)
    4. Results
    5. Comparison to original
    6. Implications for the original finding
  Description: Study explicitly attempting to replicate a previous finding — direct replication, conceptual replication, or pre-registered replication.
  Exemplars: Many Open Science Collaboration papers; Many Labs projects; modern psychology replication crisis literature.

GENRE 597: Pre-Registered Report
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Stage 1: introduction, hypotheses, method, analysis plan
    2. Reviewer feedback and accept-in-principle
    3. Stage 2: data collection and analysis
    4. Results (matching pre-registered plan)
    5. Discussion
    6. Final acceptance
  Description: Research report whose method and analysis plan were submitted and accepted for publication before data collection — protecting against p-hacking and HARKing.
  Exemplars: Center for Open Science framework; Registered Reports in psychology and biology journals.

GENRE 598: Critical Theory Essay
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Theoretical framework introduced
    2. Object of analysis chosen
    3. Application of framework
    4. Critical claims advanced
    5. Counter-positions addressed
    6. Implications for understanding
  Description: Academic essay applying a critical theoretical framework (Marxist, feminist, postcolonial, queer, deconstructive) to a text or phenomenon. Specific theoretical lens.
  Exemplars: Frankfurt School essays; Judith Butler's gender essays; Edward Said's Orientalism; bell hooks's essays.

GENRE 599: Conference Paper Abstract
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Background and gap
    2. Research question
    3. Method
    4. Findings
    5. Implications
    6. Why this conference
  Description: Brief (250-500 word) abstract submitted to a conference for review — must convey scholarly contribution in highly compressed form. Competitive-selection function.
  Exemplars: Standard academic conference submissions across disciplines; abstracts for AAAS, MLA, AMA conferences.

GENRE 600: Tenure Case Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Personal statement and trajectory
    2. Research contributions
    3. Teaching effectiveness
    4. Service and citizenship
    5. External evaluations
    6. Case for promotion
  Description: Faculty member's case for tenure assembled as research, teaching, and service narrative — designed for committee review. Career-defining stakes.
  Exemplars: Standard tenure dossiers; tenure-and-promotion narratives across U.S. higher education.

---

# EDUCATIONAL GENRES (deep)

GENRE 601: Course Syllabus
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Course title and instructor info
    2. Description and learning objectives
    3. Required materials
    4. Schedule of topics and readings
    5. Assignments and grading
    6. Policies (attendance, late work, AI use)
  Description: Document outlining a course's content, structure, expectations, and policies — distributed to students at term start. Institutional-pedagogical register.
  Exemplars: Standard university course syllabi; K-12 course outlines; online-course descriptions.

GENRE 602: Lesson Plan
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Lesson objectives
    2. Materials needed
    3. Hook or opening
    4. Direct instruction
    5. Practice or activity
    6. Assessment and closure
  Description: Detailed instructor's plan for a single class session — objectives, activities, materials, assessment. Specific-session granularity.
  Exemplars: Standard K-12 lesson plans; Madeline Hunter lesson plan model; backwards design lesson plans (Wiggins/McTighe).

GENRE 603: Curriculum Design Document
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Educational philosophy and goals
    2. Sequence of learning units
    3. Skills development progression
    4. Assessment plan
    5. Resources and texts
    6. Implementation timeline
  Description: Long-arc document designing an entire course or program of study — sequenced learning, assessment, philosophy. Design-thinking scope.
  Exemplars: Common Core curriculum; IB program documents; school-level curriculum mapping; corporate training curricula.

GENRE 604: Reading Comprehension Passage
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Passage with controlled length and difficulty
    2. Main idea and supporting details embedded
    3. Vocabulary and inference opportunities
    4. Followed by question set
  Description: Short passage designed for assessment of reading skills — typically followed by questions. Specific testing-instrument register.
  Exemplars: SAT, ACT, AP exam passages; standardized testing material; reading curricula.

GENRE 605: Worksheet / Practice Set
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Topic and instructions
    2. Worked example or warm-up
    3. Easy practice items
    4. Progressively harder items
    5. Application or word problems
    6. Answer key (often)
  Description: Set of practice problems or exercises for student practice. Drill-and-practice register and progressive difficulty.
  Exemplars: Standard math worksheets; language-learning practice sets; standardized prep books.

---

GENRE 606: Lab Report
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Title and objective
    2. Materials and apparatus
    3. Procedure
    4. Observations and data
    5. Analysis and discussion
    6. Conclusion
  Description: Student's account of a laboratory experiment — protocol, observations, conclusions. Specific empirical-pedagogical register; pre-IMRAD training.
  Exemplars: Standard secondary and undergraduate lab reports; chemistry, physics, biology lab notebooks.

GENRE 607: Field Trip Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-trip context and objectives
    2. Travel and arrival
    3. Activities and observations
    4. Specific moments of learning
    5. Reflection and discussion
    6. Application to classroom learning
  Description: Student or teacher account of educational excursion — what was seen, learned, experienced. Specific outside-classroom learning register.
  Exemplars: Class trip writeups; museum education narratives; outdoor education program accounts.

GENRE 608: Standardized Test Question
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Stem (question or prompt)
    2. Answer choices (if multiple choice)
    3. One correct answer; distractors plausible
    4. Test-taker's response
  Description: Single question on a standardized exam — multiple-choice, short-answer, or essay. Specific assessment-instrument register.
  Exemplars: SAT, ACT, GRE, MCAT items; Common Core assessments; psychometric instruments.

GENRE 609: Essay Prompt / Writing Assignment
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Topic or question
    2. Length and format requirements
    3. Audience specification
    4. Evaluation criteria
    5. Due date
    6. Resources or constraints
  Description: Instructional prompt asking students to write — typically with specific question, length, audience, and criteria. Meta-genre: a prompt that generates a genre.
  Exemplars: AP English exam prompts; college admission essay prompts; classroom writing assignments.

GENRE 610: Children's Picture Book
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Opening illustration introducing protagonist/setting
    2. Spread-by-spread story development
    3. Repetition or refrain for memorability
    4. Climactic spread (often double-page)
    5. Resolution
    6. Closing image (often warm, bedtime-appropriate)
  Description: Short narrative for young children combining illustration and text. Visual-textual integration and developmentally-appropriate register.
  Exemplars: Maurice Sendak's Where the Wild Things Are; Eric Carle's The Very Hungry Caterpillar; Mo Willems's books; Goodnight Moon.

GENRE 611: Educational Children's Book (chapter)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Engaging hook in opening chapter
    2. Series of episodic chapters
    3. Vocabulary and concept teaching woven in
    4. Climactic chapter
    5. Resolution chapter
    6. Suggested follow-on activities
  Description: Chapter book aimed at independent young readers (~7-12) — narrative shaped to be both engaging and teachingly age-appropriate. Longer chapter-divided structure.
  Exemplars: Beverly Cleary's Ramona books; Jeff Kinney's Diary of a Wimpy Kid; Magic Tree House; Nikki Grimes.

---

# PERSONAL & EMOTIONAL FORMS

GENRE 612: Apology Letter
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Acknowledgment of the wrong (specific)
    2. Acceptance of responsibility (no excuses)
    3. Recognition of impact on the recipient
    4. Concrete commitment to change
    5. No demand for forgiveness
    6. Closing in honest direct voice
  Description: Direct written acknowledgment of wrong to a specific person, accepting responsibility and seeking repair. Specific accountability register.
  Exemplars: Personal apology letters; corporate apology statements (when honest); literary examples (Augustine to his mother).

GENRE 613: Love Letter
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Direct salutation
    2. Account of feeling for the recipient
    3. Specific memories or qualities cherished
    4. Declaration of love
    5. Hope for the future
    6. Tender closing
  Description: Written declaration of romantic love or longing to a specific person. Specific intimate-direct register, often with intentional poetic heightening.
  Exemplars: John Keats's letters to Fanny Brawne; Napoleon's letters to Josephine; Frida Kahlo's love letters; Beethoven's "Immortal Beloved" letters.

GENRE 614: Dear John / Breakup Letter
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Acknowledgment of the relationship
    2. What is ending and why
    3. Recognition of what was good
    4. Reasons for ending (without blame, ideally)
    5. What both parties need now
    6. Closing
  Description: Written end-of-relationship letter to a partner. Specific termination-with-explanation register.
  Exemplars: Personal breakup letters; literary examples (The Letter; many novels' epistolary breaks).

GENRE 615: Resignation Letter
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Statement of resignation effective by date
    2. Brief acknowledgment of role
    3. Thanks for opportunity
    4. Offer to assist transition
    5. Professional closing
    6. Often: no detailed reasons given
  Description: Formal written notice of resignation from employment. Specific institutional-professional register.
  Exemplars: Standard resignation letter templates; corporate convention; public-figure resignation letters.

GENRE 616: Cover Letter (job application)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Salutation to specific person if possible
    2. Position applied for and how learned
    3. Specific qualifications matching the role
    4. Why this organization
    5. Call to action (interview request)
    6. Professional closing
  Description: Letter accompanying a job application — introducing the candidate, stating interest, summarizing qualifications. Specific job-search rhetorical register.
  Exemplars: Standard job-application cover letters; modern targeted cover letters.

GENRE 617: Recommendation Letter
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. How writer knows subject
    2. Subject's specific qualities and abilities
    3. Specific examples illustrating qualities
    4. Comparison to others writer has known
    5. Strong endorsement
    6. Offer to discuss further
  Description: Written endorsement of a person — for job, school, award. Third-party authority register; the writer's credibility transfers.
  Exemplars: Standard recommendation letters; faculty grad-school recommendations; corporate references.

GENRE 618: Sympathy / Condolence Letter
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Acknowledgment of the loss
    2. Specific memory of the deceased (if applicable)
    3. Recognition of the pain
    4. Offer of presence (without intrusion)
    5. Closing without urging "moving on"
  Description: Written expression of sympathy following another's loss. Specific compassion-without-fixing register.
  Exemplars: Standard condolence letters; Lincoln's letter to Mrs. Bixby; modern grief literature recommendations.

GENRE 619: Thank You Note
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Salutation
    2. Specific thanks for what was given
    3. How it affected the recipient
    4. Looking forward to next encounter
    5. Closing
    6. Handwritten signature (traditional)
  Description: Brief written expression of gratitude for a gift, service, or kindness. Specific concise-warm register.
  Exemplars: Standard thank-you notes; thank-you letters after job interviews; wedding-gift thank-yous.

GENRE 620: Greeting Card Message
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Greeting
    2. Specific reflection on relationship or year
    3. Wish for celebrant
    4. Affectionate close
    5. Signature
  Description: Brief celebratory message inside a greeting card. Specific warm-affectionate compressed register.
  Exemplars: Standard birthday card messages; Hallmark and modern card text; personal-customized notes.

GENRE 621: Personal Vow (wedding, commitment)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Direct address to partner
    2. Acknowledgment of who they are
    3. Promises made
    4. What love means in this commitment
    5. Future-oriented declaration
    6. Closing pledge
  Description: Words of commitment spoken at a marriage or other ceremonial bond. Specific public-promise register.
  Exemplars: Personal wedding vows; modern vow-writing templates; literary examples.

GENRE 622: Public Apology (Celebrity / Corporate)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Acknowledgment of the action
    2. Direct statement of regret
    3. Reflection on impact
    4. What is being done now
    5. Commitment to change
    6. Closing without minimizing
  Description: Public statement of apology by celebrity or organization for action that drew public criticism. Public audience and image-management stakes.
  Exemplars: Domino's Pizza response (2009); Tiger Woods's public apology; many modern celebrity apologies (success and failure cases).

GENRE 623: Personal Statement (admissions)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Opening hook (often anecdote)
    2. Background and trajectory
    3. Specific moment of formation or decision
    4. Why this program/opportunity
    5. What you will contribute
    6. Closing tied to opening
  Description: First-person narrative submitted to admissions committees — college, grad school, fellowships. High-stakes self-presentation register.
  Exemplars: College admission essays; med school personal statements; fellowship applications (Rhodes, Marshall).

GENRE 624: Last Letter (deathbed / parting)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Acknowledgment of writing-from-the-end
    2. Love and gratitude expressed
    3. Specific memories or wishes
    4. What the writer hopes for survivors
    5. Practical instructions if any
    6. Closing benediction
  Description: Letter written knowing the writer is dying or leaving permanently — addressed to family, beloved, or community. Terminal stakes register.
  Exemplars: Sullivan Ballou's letter before Bull Run; deathbed letters in literature; military "in case of my death" letters.

GENRE 625: Personal Manifesto
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Statement of values
    2. Current state of one's life
    3. What is being committed to
    4. What is being released
    5. Specific action principles
    6. Closing pledge
  Description: Personal declaration of values, goals, and stances written for self-clarification (or sometimes public). Individual-life register.
  Exemplars: Personal manifestos in self-help literature; founder personal statements; year-end review documents.

GENRE 626: Coming Out Letter / Statement
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Direct address
    2. Identity statement
    3. Reflection on what it means to share this
    4. What hasn't changed
    5. What is hoped for in response
    6. Affirmation of love
  Description: Written disclosure of LGBTQ+ identity to family, friends, or public. Specific identity-disclosure register and stakes.
  Exemplars: Coming-out letters in memoirs; published examples (Anderson Cooper's); modern coming-out support literature.

# REALITY / NON-FICTION TV & MODERN AUDIOVISUAL

GENRE 627: Reality Competition Show
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Contestants introduced with backstories
    2. Challenge announced
    3. Contestants compete
    4. Judges deliberate
    5. Elimination
    6. Cliffhanger to next episode
  Description: Competition format with elimination across episodes — cooking, design, dating, athletic. Specific competitive-elimination structure.
  Exemplars: Survivor; Top Chef; RuPaul's Drag Race; The Bachelor; Project Runway.

GENRE 628: Talk Show (interview-based)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Monologue or opening
    2. First guest brought out
    3. Interview Q&A
    4. Segment break/musical performance
    5. Subsequent guests
    6. Closing
  Description: Television format featuring host interviewing guests — late-night, daytime, news, or specialty. Host-guest dynamic structure.
  Exemplars: The Tonight Show; The Daily Show; Oprah; Hot Ones (modern variant).

GENRE 629: Game Show
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Host introduces show and contestants
    2. Rules explained
    3. Rounds of game play
    4. Eliminations or scoring
    5. Final round/jackpot
    6. Winner announced
  Description: Television format with contestants competing in games for prizes. Specific game-mechanics structure.
  Exemplars: Jeopardy!; Wheel of Fortune; The Price Is Right; Family Feud.

GENRE 630: Documentary Series (long-form)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Episode 1 establishes subject
    2. Each episode explores facet/period
    3. Recurring narrator/structure
    4. Climactic episode
    5. Resolution episode
    6. Reflection across the series
  Description: Multi-episode documentary on a single subject — true crime, history, sports, biography. Serial scope.
  Exemplars: The Last Dance (Jordan); Tiger King; Making a Murderer; Wild Wild Country; The Vietnam War (Burns).

GENRE 631: True Crime Documentary
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Crime introduced
    2. Victim humanized
    3. Investigation traced
    4. Suspects and witnesses
    5. Resolution (or its absence)
    6. Lasting questions
  Description: Documentary series or feature specifically investigating real crimes. Specific genre commitment.
  Exemplars: Making a Murderer; The Jinx; Don't F**k With Cats; The Staircase; Conversations with a Killer.

GENRE 632: Nature / Wildlife Documentary
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Ecosystem or species introduced
    2. Behavior or life cycle observed
    3. Challenges and adaptations
    4. Climactic event (hunt, migration, birth)
    5. Conservation context
    6. Future outlook
  Description: Documentary about natural world — animals, ecosystems, geography. Natural-world subject and conservation-adjacent register.
  Exemplars: Planet Earth; Blue Planet; Our Planet; David Attenborough's productions; Tiger (Disneynature).

GENRE 633: Cooking Show (instructional)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Host introduces dish
    2. Ingredients and equipment
    3. Step-by-step preparation
    4. Tips and variations
    5. Plating and tasting
    6. Recipe summary
  Description: TV format demonstrating recipes and cooking techniques. Instructional non-competitive register.
  Exemplars: Julia Child's The French Chef; Good Eats (Alton Brown); Barefoot Contessa; many YouTube cooking shows.

GENRE 634: Travel Show
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Destination introduced
    2. Host's first impressions
    3. Specific cultural encounters
    4. Local food and characters
    5. Climactic experience
    6. Departure with reflection
  Description: TV format following host(s) traveling to destinations — culture, food, adventure. Host-as-traveler register and place-focused structure.
  Exemplars: Anthony Bourdain's Parts Unknown and No Reservations; Rick Steves's travel; Conan Without Borders; Stanley Tucci's Searching for Italy.

GENRE 635: Home Renovation / Makeover Show
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Subject introduced with current state
    2. Project envisioned
    3. Demolition or stripping
    4. Construction or transformation
    5. Climactic reveal
    6. Subject's reaction and new life
  Description: TV format featuring transformation of home, person, or space — before-and-after structure. Specific physical-transformation register.
  Exemplars: Queer Eye; Fixer Upper; Extreme Makeover: Home Edition; Tidying Up with Marie Kondo.

GENRE 636: Sketch Comedy
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Cold open / opening sketch
    2. Series of distinct sketches
    3. Recurring characters across season
    4. Musical guest break
    5. Closing sketch
    6. Goodnights
  Description: TV format featuring multiple short comedic skits, typically 2-7 minutes each. Episodic-self-contained-bit structure.
  Exemplars: Saturday Night Live; Monty Python's Flying Circus; Key & Peele; I Think You Should Leave; Mr. Show.

# SPORTS NARRATIVES

GENRE 637: Championship Run Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Season opens with goals
    2. Regular season trials
    3. Playoff entry
    4. Each round's challenges
    5. Final championship
    6. Aftermath and legacy
  Description: Narrative tracking a team or individual's run through a championship season or playoff. Specific season-arc register with elimination stakes.
  Exemplars: The Last Dance (Bulls); coverage of any major championship run; Friday Night Lights (Bissinger).

GENRE 638: Comeback Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Past achievement and identity
    2. Fall (injury, defeat, retirement)
    3. Lowest point
    4. Decision to return
    5. Training and rebuilding
    6. Climactic triumph or honored loss
  Description: Sports story of athlete or team returning from injury, retirement, or defeat to triumph. Specific redemption-through-sport register.
  Exemplars: Tiger Woods's 2019 Masters; Andre Agassi's career arc; Rocky II; many sports comeback stories.

GENRE 639: Athlete Career Retrospective
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Origins and youth
    2. Discovery or development of talent
    3. Rise to prominence
    4. Peak achievements
    5. Decline or retirement
    6. Place in the sport's history
  Description: Long-form narrative of an athlete's complete career — origins, peak, decline, legacy. Specifically athletic frame.
  Exemplars: Andre Agassi's Open; David Maraniss's When Pride Still Mattered (Lombardi); Roger Kahn's The Boys of Summer; The Last Dance.

GENRE 640: Single-Game Reconstruction
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-game context and stakes
    2. Pre-game narratives of key players
    3. Game beat-by-beat
    4. Decisive moment
    5. Post-game reaction
    6. Lasting significance
  Description: Detailed narrative of a single sporting event — moment by moment, with backstory and context. Book-length depth.
  Exemplars: Books on Game 7 of various series; The Boys of Summer's game accounts; Mark Bowden's Bringing the Heat; Friday Night Lights game chapters.

GENRE 641: Sports Team Profile
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Team and place introduced
    2. Coach and key players profiled
    3. Season's arc
    4. Internal tensions and bonds
    5. Climactic moment
    6. What this team means
  Description: Long-form portrait of a sports team — its players, coach, culture, season. Specifically team-as-character register.
  Exemplars: Friday Night Lights (Bissinger); The Boys of Summer (Kahn); David Halberstam's basketball books; The Last Dance.

GENRE 642: Sports Rivalry Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Rivalry origin
    2. First major confrontation
    3. Each subsequent encounter
    4. Defining moment
    5. Late-career meeting
    6. Reflection on what the rivalry meant
  Description: Narrative tracking the long-running rivalry between two teams or athletes. Specifically multi-encounter, head-to-head structure.
  Exemplars: Bird-Magic rivalry; Federer-Nadal-Djokovic; Yankees-Red Sox; Real Madrid-Barcelona.

GENRE 643: Coaching Memoir / Strategy Book
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Coach's playing background
    2. Coaching philosophy formation
    3. Specific seasons and games
    4. Lessons learned
    5. Defining championships or losses
    6. Application beyond sport
  Description: Coach's first-person account of methods, philosophy, and seasons. Specifically strategic-leadership register.
  Exemplars: Phil Jackson's Sacred Hoops; John Wooden's books; Bill Walsh's The Score Takes Care of Itself; Pat Summitt's memoirs.

GENRE 644: Sports Documentary Feature
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Subject introduced
    2. Backstory
    3. Specific season or career
    4. Defining moment
    5. Aftermath
    6. Legacy and reflection
  Description: Feature-length documentary on athlete, team, or sporting event. Specific filmic-sports register.
  Exemplars: Hoop Dreams; When We Were Kings; O.J.: Made in America; Senna; Free Solo.

GENRE 645: Underdog Sports Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Underdog team/athlete introduced
    2. Established underestimation
    3. Initial losses or doubts
    4. Coach or moment that shifts
    5. Surprising playoff/match run
    6. Climactic upset victory
  Description: Sports story specifically of unlikely team or athlete defeating favored opposition. Sports-specific underdog arc.
  Exemplars: Hoosiers; Miracle on Ice (1980 USA hockey); Rocky; Leicester City's 2015-16 EPL title; Cool Runnings.

GENRE 646: Adventure Sport / Extreme Memoir
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Sport and personal history
    2. Specific feat or attempt
    3. Training and preparation
    4. Attempt itself
    5. Climactic moment of danger or success
    6. What was learned
  Description: First-person account of extreme sport feat — climbing, ultra-running, sailing, free diving. Adrenaline-stakes register.
  Exemplars: Aron Ralston's Between a Rock and a Hard Place; Free Solo (Honnold); David Roberts; Joe Simpson's Touching the Void.

---

# WORK / CAREER NARRATIVES

GENRE 647: Founder Story
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-founding life
    2. Idea and decision to found
    3. Early struggles
    4. Specific breakthroughs and pivots
    5. Scaling and trials
    6. Present state and reflection
  Description: Long-form narrative of a startup founder — origin, struggles, breakthroughs. Book-length scope.
  Exemplars: Phil Knight's Shoe Dog; Howard Schultz's Pour Your Heart Into It; Walter Isaacson's Steve Jobs; Bad Blood (Holmes/Theranos — variant).

GENRE 648: Career Pivot Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. First career and growing dissatisfaction
    2. Crisis or trigger
    3. Decision to leave
    4. Reskilling or starting over
    5. Initial struggles in new path
    6. Settled second-act identity
  Description: Narrative of changing careers mid-life — abandoning one trajectory for another. Specific second-act register.
  Exemplars: Po Bronson's What Should I Do With My Life?; Cheryl Strayed's Wild; many Substack newsletter founder stories.

GENRE 649: Quitting / Resignation Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Position and tenure context
    2. Decision to leave
    3. Reasons (often principled)
    4. What was learned
    5. What follows
    6. Address to those still inside
  Description: Public account of leaving a job, organization, or institution — typically with critique or stand-taking. Public-narrative register.
  Exemplars: Resignation essays in The Atlantic; Bari Weiss's NYT resignation letter; published whistleblower departures.

GENRE 650: Performance Review (employee)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Period reviewed
    2. Goals and outcomes
    3. Strengths demonstrated
    4. Development areas
    5. Compensation/promotion implications
    6. Forward goals
  Description: Manager's structured assessment of employee performance — typically annual. Specific evaluation-development register.
  Exemplars: Standard corporate performance reviews; 360 reviews; modern continuous-feedback variants.

GENRE 651: 360-Degree Review
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Input from multiple stakeholders
    2. Themes synthesized
    3. Strengths consensus
    4. Development consensus
    5. Discussion with reviewee
    6. Action plan
  Description: Performance review gathering input from manager, peers, direct reports, and self. Multi-source structure.
  Exemplars: Modern corporate 360 review processes; coaching applications of 360 feedback.

GENRE 652: Self-Assessment / Self-Review
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Period reviewed
    2. Goals and self-assessment of progress
    3. Specific accomplishments
    4. Self-identified development areas
    5. Goals for next period
    6. Career aspirations
  Description: Employee's own structured assessment of their performance — accompanies or precedes manager review. First-person professional reflection register.
  Exemplars: Standard self-review documents; corporate self-assessment forms.

GENRE 653: Promotion Case
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Current role and tenure
    2. Performance against expectations
    3. Demonstrated next-level capabilities
    4. Specific evidence (projects, scope)
    5. Recommendation
    6. Compensation implications
  Description: Document advocating for an employee's promotion — combining manager case, employee accomplishments, peer evidence. Specific advancement-advocacy register.
  Exemplars: Standard promotion-case documents in modern tech; FAANG promotion convention; consulting-firm partner cases.

GENRE 654: Resume / CV
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Contact info
    2. Summary or objective
    3. Experience (reverse chronological)
    4. Education
    5. Skills
    6. Additional sections (publications, certifications)
  Description: Compressed document listing professional history, education, and skills. Highly conventional, scannable structure.
  Exemplars: Standard resume templates; CV formats (academic); LinkedIn profiles (modern variant).

GENRE 655: LinkedIn Post / Professional Reflection
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Hook (often personal or counterintuitive)
    2. Specific story or moment
    3. Lesson distilled
    4. Application to broader audience
    5. Call to engagement
    6. Hashtags
  Description: Short professional reflection or update posted on LinkedIn — career milestone, lesson, opinion. Specific platform-conventionalized register.
  Exemplars: Standard LinkedIn long-posts; "3 things I learned" format; modern professional thought-leadership content.

GENRE 656: Interview Story (job interview)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Situation — context briefly set
    2. Task — what was needed
    3. Action — what the candidate did
    4. Result — outcome and metric
    5. Reflection — what was learned
    6. Application to the role
  Description: Narrative-format interview answer — STAR (Situation, Task, Action, Result) or similar structure. Specific demonstration-of-competency register.
  Exemplars: Standard behavioral-interview answers; modern interview-prep frameworks (STAR, CAR).

GENRE 657: Onboarding Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. New hire's first days
    2. Orientation and introductions
    3. First project or task
    4. Initial mistakes and learning
    5. Building relationships
    6. Settled integration
  Description: First-person account of joining an organization — first weeks, learning the culture, finding footing. Specific entry-into-institution register.
  Exemplars: Modern onboarding-experience blog posts; First, Break All the Rules onboarding chapters; "my first 90 days at..." Substack posts.

GENRE 658: Exit Interview Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Reason for leaving
    2. What worked in the role
    3. What didn't work
    4. Feedback for the organization
    5. Future plans
    6. Closing thoughts
  Description: Structured conversation with departing employee — capturing institutional learning. Specific candor-at-departure register.
  Exemplars: Standard HR exit interviews; published exit-interview essays; modern offboarding practices.

GENRE 659: Career Coaching Conversation
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Current career state
    2. Aspirations and what's blocking them
    3. Strengths and underleveraged talents
    4. Possible paths explored
    5. Next steps committed to
    6. Accountability check-in
  Description: Structured coaching dialogue focused on career direction — distinguished from generic mentorship by specifically career-trajectory focus.
  Exemplars: Standard executive coaching career conversations; career-counselor sessions; Designing Your Life (Burnett/Evans) framework.

GENRE 660: Networking Pitch / Elevator Pitch
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Who I am (one line)
    2. What I do (specifically)
    3. What makes it interesting or unique
    4. What I'm looking for / next step
  Description: Compressed self-introduction designed for networking events, conferences, or chance encounters — typically 30-60 seconds. Specific concise self-presentation register.
  Exemplars: Standard elevator pitches; networking event introductions; conference badge-up small-talk; modern "tell me about yourself" answers.

---

# RELATIONSHIP / HEALTH NARRATIVES

GENRE 661: Divorce Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Marriage that mattered
    2. Cracks emerging
    3. Decision to divorce
    4. Process and pain
    5. Aftermath and rebuilding
    6. New equilibrium
  Description: Memoir or essay processing the experience of divorce. Specific dissolution-of-marriage register.
  Exemplars: Nora Ephron's Heartburn; Rachel Cusk's Aftermath; Eat Pray Love (in part); many divorce memoirs.

GENRE 662: Grief Memoir
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-death life with the deceased
    2. Death itself
    3. Acute grief
    4. Long arc of mourning
    5. Reconfiguration of life without the person
    6. What grief reveals
  Description: First-person narrative of mourning a specific loss — spouse, child, parent, friend. Specific bereavement-process register.
  Exemplars: Joan Didion's The Year of Magical Thinking; C.S. Lewis's A Grief Observed; Joyce Carol Oates's A Widow's Story; Helen Macdonald's H Is for Hawk.

GENRE 663: Diagnosis Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-diagnosis life
    2. Symptoms or anomalies
    3. Tests and uncertainty
    4. Diagnosis received
    5. Treatment begins
    6. Adjusting to new reality
  Description: Narrative of receiving a serious medical diagnosis — illness named, life reorganized, treatment begun. Specific medical-life-pivot register.
  Exemplars: Susan Sontag's Illness as Metaphor; Paul Kalanithi's When Breath Becomes Air; Anatole Broyard's Intoxicated by My Illness; Audre Lorde's The Cancer Journals.

GENRE 664: Recovery Narrative (health)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Health crisis or surgery
    2. Initial recovery and limitations
    3. Setbacks
    4. Rehabilitation work
    5. Climactic milestone
    6. New relationship with body and mortality
  Description: Narrative of recovery from serious illness, injury, or surgery. Medical-physical register distinct from spiritual recovery.
  Exemplars: Atul Gawande's essays; Christopher Reeve's Still Me; My Stroke of Insight (Bolte Taylor); cancer recovery memoirs.

GENRE 665: Addiction / Recovery Memoir
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-addiction life
    2. First use and seductive period
    3. Addiction takes over
    4. Bottom
    5. Decision to recover; treatment/program
    6. Sustained sobriety and ongoing struggle
  Description: Memoir of substance addiction and recovery. Specific addiction-substance register.
  Exemplars: Caroline Knapp's Drinking: A Love Story; Mary Karr's Lit; Augusten Burroughs's Dry; James Frey's A Million Little Pieces (controversial).

GENRE 666: Mental Health Memoir
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-illness life
    2. Onset of mental illness
    3. Crisis and diagnosis
    4. Treatment journey
    5. Stabilization
    6. Living with the condition
  Description: First-person narrative of mental illness, treatment, and integration. Specific psychiatric-experience register.
  Exemplars: William Styron's Darkness Visible; Kay Redfield Jamison's An Unquiet Mind; Susanna Kaysen's Girl, Interrupted; Andrew Solomon's The Noonday Demon.

GENRE 667: Trauma Recovery Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Life before trauma
    2. Traumatic event
    3. Acute aftermath
    4. Post-traumatic difficulty
    5. Therapeutic work
    6. Integration and ongoing healing
  Description: Narrative of processing and recovering from trauma — abuse, assault, accident. Specific trauma-integration register.
  Exemplars: Lucy Grealy's Autobiography of a Face; Sherman Alexie; Roxane Gay's Hunger; Chanel Miller's Know My Name.

GENRE 668: Caregiver Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Loved one's diagnosis or decline
    2. Decision to care
    3. Caregiving routine
    4. Specific challenges and moments of grace
    5. Climactic medical event or death
    6. Reflection on what caregiving meant
  Description: First-person account of caring for a sick, aging, or dying family member. Specific caregiver-position register.
  Exemplars: Roz Chast's Can't We Talk About Something More Pleasant?; Atul Gawande's Being Mortal; Sue Miller's The Story of My Father.

GENRE 669: Adoption / Birth Family Search Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Adoption context
    2. Question or longing for biological identity
    3. Search begins
    4. Discoveries through records, DNA, contacts
    5. Climactic meeting
    6. Reckoning with what was found
  Description: Narrative of adoptee's search for birth family or birth family's search for placed child. Specific identity-and-origin search register.
  Exemplars: Jeanette Winterson's Why Be Happy When You Could Be Normal?; Dani Shapiro's Inheritance; A.M. Homes's The Mistress's Daughter; modern DNA-discovery memoirs.

GENRE 670: Family Estrangement Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Family relationship history
    2. Long-running tensions
    3. Triggering event
    4. Decision to estrange
    5. Aftermath and grief
    6. Living with the absence
  Description: Narrative of cutting off a family member or being cut off — typically with detailed reasoning and emotional cost. Specific family-rupture register.
  Exemplars: Jeanette Winterson; Justine Bateman's writings; modern essays on parental estrangement; many trauma memoirs.

GENRE 671: Parenting Memoir
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-parent self
    2. Pregnancy or adoption
    3. Birth or arrival
    4. Specific challenges of early years
    5. Moments of revelation
    6. Reflection on what parenthood teaches
  Description: First-person narrative of becoming and being a parent. Specific parental-experience register.
  Exemplars: Anne Lamott's Operating Instructions; Andrew Solomon's Far from the Tree; Rivka Galchen's Little Labors; many modern parenting memoirs.

GENRE 672: Friendship Memoir / Eulogy
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Friendship's beginning
    2. Period of closeness
    3. Specific shared experiences
    4. Distance, change, or loss
    5. What the friendship gave
    6. What lingers
  Description: First-person account of a particular friendship and what it meant. Friendship as central frame.
  Exemplars: Michelle Tea's writings; Roxane Gay on Roxana; many essay collections featuring friendship memoir; Tuesdays with Morrie (variant).

GENRE 673: Patient Story (medical communication)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-diagnosis health
    2. Encounter with healthcare system
    3. Specific challenges navigating care
    4. Treatment experience
    5. What patients want healthcare to know
    6. Implications for system change
  Description: First-person account of a patient's medical experience, often used in medical education or advocacy. Specific medical-system perspective.
  Exemplars: Patient stories in NEJM's "Perspective"; Health Affairs narratives; advocacy organization patient stories.

GENRE 674: Doctor-Writer Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Clinical situation introduced
    2. Patient encountered
    3. Diagnostic or treatment puzzle
    4. Decision and action
    5. Outcome and reflection
    6. What this case teaches
  Description: Physician's first-person account of clinical practice — patients, system, ethical dilemmas. Distinct from patient story by physician's perspective and authority.
  Exemplars: Atul Gawande's essays; Oliver Sacks's case studies; Abraham Verghese; Lisa Sanders's Diagnosis column; Siddhartha Mukherjee.

GENRE 675: Therapy Session Reconstruction
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Therapist's frame for the patient
    2. Session opens
    3. Patient brings material
    4. Therapeutic intervention or insight
    5. Patient's response
    6. Therapist's reflection
  Description: Narrative reconstruction of psychotherapy sessions — specific therapeutic-encounter register. Common in popular psychology writing.
  Exemplars: Irvin Yalom's Love's Executioner; Stephen Grosz's The Examined Life; Lori Gottlieb's Maybe You Should Talk to Someone.

---

# WAR & MILITARY NARRATIVES

GENRE 676: War Memoir (combat)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Pre-war life
    2. Enlistment and training
    3. Deployment and first combat
    4. Specific events of war
    5. Climactic action or wound
    6. Return and integration
  Description: First-person account of combat experience by a veteran. Specific combatant-perspective register.
  Exemplars: Tim O'Brien's The Things They Carried (in part); Karl Marlantes's What It Is Like to Go to War; Phil Klay's Redeployment; Erich Maria Remarque's All Quiet on the Western Front.

GENRE 677: War Correspondence / Front-Line Reportage
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Correspondent in zone
    2. Specific incident witnessed
    3. Soldiers and civilians spoken with
    4. Larger war context provided
    5. Climactic event
    6. Filing the story
  Description: Journalistic reporting from active war zones. Specific reportorial-from-combat register.
  Exemplars: Ernie Pyle's WWII columns; Michael Herr's Dispatches (Vietnam); Anna Politkovskaya's Chechnya reporting; Marie Colvin's reports; Sebastian Junger's War.

GENRE 678: Battle Narrative (historical)
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Strategic context before battle
    2. Forces and commanders
    3. Battle plan
    4. Combat unfolds (tactical detail)
    5. Decisive moments
    6. Aftermath and significance
  Description: Detailed reconstruction of a historical battle — strategy, tactics, individual stories. Specific military-historical scope.
  Exemplars: Stephen Ambrose's Citizen Soldiers; Cornelius Ryan's The Longest Day; James M. McPherson's Civil War battle narratives; Antony Beevor's WWII books.

GENRE 679: Resistance Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Occupation or oppression establishes
    2. Decision to resist
    3. Resistance network or solo action
    4. Specific operations
    5. Capture, betrayal, or success
    6. Legacy of resistance
  Description: Narrative of resistance against occupation, oppression, or tyranny. Specific underground-defiance register.
  Exemplars: Anne Frank's Diary; Hans Fallada's Every Man Dies Alone; Schindler's List (Keneally); WWII French Resistance narratives; Inglourious Basterds (fictional variant).

GENRE 680: POW Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Capture
    2. Prison camp conditions
    3. Daily survival and resistance
    4. Specific incidents (escape attempts, deaths, torture)
    5. Liberation or escape
    6. Return and reflection
  Description: Account of imprisonment as prisoner of war. Specific captivity-and-survival register.
  Exemplars: Louis Zamperini's story (Unbroken, Hillenbrand); Eugene Sledge's With the Old Breed; Stephen Ambrose; Boris Pasternak prisoner-themes.

GENRE 681: Military Strategic Analysis
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Strategic question
    2. Historical or current context
    3. Forces and capabilities
    4. Strategic options analyzed
    5. Recommended approach
    6. Implications for doctrine
  Description: Analytical writing on military strategy, doctrine, or campaign. Prescriptive-analytical register distinct from history.
  Exemplars: Carl von Clausewitz's On War; modern RAND reports; war college analyses; Foreign Affairs strategy pieces.

GENRE 682: Veteran Reintegration Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Return from deployment
    2. Initial reentry
    3. PTSD or alienation
    4. Search for identity
    5. Help sought (or not)
    6. Sustained civilian life or continued struggle
  Description: Account of veteran's return to civilian life — psychological, social, professional reintegration. Specific post-deployment register.
  Exemplars: Brian Turner's My Life as a Foreign Country; American Sniper (post-war material); Kevin Powers's The Yellow Birds; Karl Marlantes.

GENRE 683: Unit History / Military Group Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Unit formed
    2. Training
    3. First deployment
    4. Major engagements
    5. Notable members
    6. Dissolution or current state
  Description: Narrative tracing a specific military unit through its formation, deployments, and dissolution. Specifically collective-unit register.
  Exemplars: Stephen Ambrose's Band of Brothers (Easy Company); James McPherson's regimental histories; modern military unit chronicles.

GENRE 684: Counterinsurgency / Asymmetric Warfare Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Conflict context (occupation, civil war)
    2. Insurgency or counterinsurgency operations
    3. Civilian populations caught
    4. Moral ambiguity
    5. Climactic engagement
    6. Lasting cost and uncertainty
  Description: Narrative of unconventional warfare — guerilla fighters, occupation, counterinsurgency. Specific asymmetric register.
  Exemplars: Sebastian Junger's War; Restrepo (documentary); Generation Kill (Wright); The Outpost (Tapper); Vietnam-era counterinsurgency literature.

GENRE 685: Atrocity / War Crime Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Context and lead-up
    2. The atrocity itself (often by careful telling)
    3. Survivors' testimony
    4. Investigation or recognition
    5. Justice or its denial
    6. Implications for memory and prevention
  Description: Narrative documenting wartime atrocities — by witness, survivor, investigator, or perpetrator. Specific moral-witness register.
  Exemplars: Philip Gourevitch's We Wish to Inform You That Tomorrow We Will Be Killed With Our Families (Rwanda); Hiroshima (Hersey); My Lai accounts; The Things They Carried episodes.

---

# TRAVEL, FOOD, SCIENCE, ENVIRONMENTAL

GENRE 686: Adventure Travel Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Adventure conceived
    2. Preparation
    3. Setting out
    4. Encounters with terrain and danger
    5. Climactic challenge
    6. Return changed
  Description: Travel narrative emphasizing physical adventure — climbing, sailing, jungle expedition. Distinguished from generic travel by danger-stakes register.
  Exemplars: Jon Krakauer's Into the Wild and Into Thin Air; Sebastian Junger's The Perfect Storm; Tim Cahill's adventure travel.

GENRE 687: Cultural Travel Essay
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Place introduced
    2. Cultural specifics observed
    3. Encounters with people
    4. Misunderstandings and breakthroughs
    5. Insight reached
    6. Departure with new understanding
  Description: Travel writing focused on encountering specific culture, language, or place. Anthropological-essayistic register distinct from adventure.
  Exemplars: Pico Iyer's travel writing; Bruce Chatwin; Paul Theroux; Sara Wheeler; Granta travel issues.

GENRE 688: Volunteer / Service Travel Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Decision to serve
    2. Arrival and placement
    3. Initial cultural friction
    4. Specific work or relationships
    5. Climactic moment of service
    6. Return with critical reflection
  Description: Travel narrative of volunteer or service work in another place — Peace Corps, NGO, missionary. Service-engagement register; often interrogating motives.
  Exemplars: Sarah Erdman's Nine Hills to Nambonkaha; Shantaram (variant); modern Peace Corps memoirs; critical NGO-experience writings.

GENRE 689: Place-Based Memoir
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Place introduced as protagonist
    2. Author's history with place
    3. Specific changes in the place
    4. What the place has shaped
    5. Climactic moment in the place
    6. Reflection on place and self
  Description: Memoir centered on the author's relationship to a specific place over time — neighborhood, region, landscape. Place as primary frame.
  Exemplars: Annie Dillard's An American Childhood; Eudora Welty's One Writer's Beginnings; Robert Macfarlane; Helen Macdonald's Vesper Flights.

GENRE 690: Restaurant Review
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Restaurant identified and contextualized
    2. Atmosphere and arrival
    3. Service description
    4. Food described and evaluated
    5. Value and appropriateness
    6. Final recommendation with stars or rating
  Description: Critical review of a restaurant — atmosphere, service, food, value. Specific dining-evaluation register.
  Exemplars: NYT restaurant reviews (Pete Wells); Michelin Guide; Eater; classical restaurant criticism.

GENRE 691: Recipe Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Personal/cultural story behind the dish
    2. Why this recipe matters now
    3. Ingredients list
    4. Step-by-step instructions
    5. Tips and variations
    6. Closing reflection
  Description: Recipe presented within a personal/cultural narrative frame — origin story, family memory, cultural context — followed by the actual recipe instructions.
  Exemplars: Modern food blogs (Smitten Kitchen); Samin Nosrat's Salt Fat Acid Heat; Food52; NYT Cooking; many ethnic-food cookbooks.

GENRE 692: Cookbook Narrative
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Author's voice and frame established
    2. Philosophy of cooking introduced
    3. Sections by ingredient/region/technique
    4. Recipes accompanied by personal-narrative paratext
    5. Climactic dish or chapter
    6. Closing reflection
  Description: Long-form cookbook with sustained narrative, philosophy, or biographical thread alongside recipes. Narrative ambition distinct from recipe-collection cookbook.
  Exemplars: Julia Child's Mastering the Art of French Cooking; Marcella Hazan; Edna Lewis's The Taste of Country Cooking; Yotam Ottolenghi's books; Madhur Jaffrey.

GENRE 693: Hunger / Body Memoir
  Tradition: Universal
  Laws (beats):
    1. Early relationship with food and body
    2. Onset of disorder or rupture
    3. Lowest point
    4. Treatment or insight
    5. Ongoing reckoning
    6. Where the relationship sits now
  Description: Memoir centered on the author's complicated relationship with food, body, hunger. Specifically embodied-disordered register.
  Exemplars: Roxane Gay's Hunger; Caroline Knapp's Appetites; Marya Hornbacher's Wasted; An Apple a Day (Emma Woolf).
